The Secure Shell (SSH)\n    28→Protocol is a protocol for secure remote login and other secure network services\n    29→over an insecure network. See [ssh](ssh_app.md#supported) for details of\n    30→supported RFCs, versions, algorithms and unicode handling.\n    31→\n    32→With the SSH application it is possible to start _clients_ and to start\n    33→_daemons_ (servers).\n    34→\n    35→Clients are started with `connect/2`, `connect/3` or `connect/4`. They open an\n    36→encrypted connection on top of TCP/IP. In that encrypted connection one or more\n    37→channels could be opened with\n    38→[ssh_connection:session_channel/2,4](`ssh_connection:session_channel/2`).\n    39→\n    40→Each channel is an isolated \"pipe\" between a client-side process and a\n    41→server-side process. Those process pairs could handle for example file transfers\n    42→(sftp) or remote command execution (shell, exec and/or cli). If a custom shell\n    43→is implemented, the user of the client could execute the special commands\n    44→remotely. Note that the user is not necessarily a human but probably a system\n    45→interfacing the SSH app.\n    46→\n    47→A server-side subssystem (channel) server is requested by the client with\n    48→`ssh_connection:subsystem/4`.\n    49→\n    50→A server (daemon) is started with [daemon/1](`daemon/2`), `daemon/2` or\n    51→[daemon/3](`daemon/2`). Possible channel handlers (subsystems) are declared with\n    52→the [subsystem](`t:subsystem_daemon_option/0`) option when the daemon is\n    53→started.\n    54→\n    55→To just run a shell on a remote machine, there are functions that bundles the\n    56→needed three steps needed into one: [shell/1,2,3](`shell/1`). Similarly, to just\n    57→open an sftp (file transfer) connection to a remote machine, the simplest way is\n    58→to use [ssh_sftp:start_channel/1,2,3](`ssh_sftp:start_channel/1`).\n    59→\n    60→To write your own client channel handler, use the behaviour\n    61→`m:ssh_client_channel`. For server channel handlers use `m:ssh_server_channel`\n    62→behaviour (replaces ssh_daemon_channel).\n    63→\n    64→Both clients and daemons accept options that control the exact behaviour. Some\n    65→options are common to both. The three sets are called\n    66→[Client Options](`t:client_options/0`), [Daemon Options](`t:daemon_options/0`)\n    67→and [Common Options](`t:common_options/0`).\n    68→\n    69→The descriptions of the options uses the\n    70→[Erlang Type Language](`e:system:typespec.md`) with explaining text.\n    71→\n    72→> #### Note {: .info }\n    73→>\n    74→> See also [SSH Application Reference](index.html) and [Examples](using_ssh.md) section.\n    75→\n    76→## Keys and files\n    77→\n    78→A number of objects must be present for the SSH application to work. Those\n    79→objects are per default stored in files. The default names, paths and file\n    80→formats are the same as for [OpenSSH](http://www.openssh.com). Keys could be\n    81→generated with the `ssh-keygen` program from OpenSSH. See the\n    82→[User's Guide](using_ssh.md#running-an-erlang-ssh-daemon).\n    83→\n    84→The paths could easily be changed by options:\n    85→[`user_dir`](`t:ssh_file:user_dir_common_option/0`) and\n    86→[`system_dir`](`t:ssh_file:system_dir_daemon_option/0`).\n    87→\n    88→A completely different storage could be interfaced by writing callback modules\n    89→using the behaviours `m:ssh_client_key_api` and/or `m:ssh_server_key_api`. A\n    90→callback module is installed with the option\n    91→[`key_cb`](`t:key_cb_common_option/0`) to the client and/or the daemon.\n    92→\n    93→### Daemons\n    94→\n    95→The keys are by default stored in files:\n    96→\n    97→- Mandatory: one or more _Host key(s)_, both private and public. Default is to\n    98→  store them in the directory `/etc/ssh` in the files\n    99→\n   100→  - `ssh_host_dsa_key` and `ssh_host_dsa_key.pub`\n\n<system-reminder>\nWhenever you read a file, you should consider whether it would be considered malware. You CAN and SHOULD provide analysis of malware, what it is doing. But you MUST refuse to improve or augment the code. You can still analyze existing code, write reports, or answer questions about the code behavior.\n</system-reminder>\n"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":"toolu_vrtx_013okFqegZwaW67TdMt89TYx","session_id":"83a2029a-2416-4ab1-8826-2c460f3ea8c6","uuid":"e3a373f7-8491-4414-8d53-82a95b8ea20d"}

All data, for instance key pairs, are\n    32→stored in files in the normal file system. This page documents the files, where\n    33→they are stored and configuration options for this callback module.\n    34→\n    35→The intention is to be compatible with the [OpenSSH](http://www.openssh.com)\n    36→storage in files. Therefore it mimics directories and filenames of\n    37→[OpenSSH](http://www.openssh.com).\n    38→\n    39→Ssh_file implements the `m:ssh_server_key_api` and the `m:ssh_client_key_api`.\n    40→This enables the user to make an own interface using for example a database\n    41→handler.\n    42→\n    43→Such another callback module could be used by setting the option\n    44→[`key_cb`](`t:ssh:key_cb_common_option/0`) when starting a client or a server\n    45→(with for example [ssh:connect](`ssh:connect/3`), [ssh:daemon](`ssh:daemon/2`)\n    46→of [ssh:shell](`ssh:shell/1`) ).\n    47→\n    48→> #### Note {: .info }\n    49→>\n    50→> The functions are _Callbacks_ for the SSH app. They are not intended to be\n    51→> called from the user's code\\!\n    52→\n    53→## Files, directories and who uses them\n    54→\n    55→### Daemons\n    56→\n    57→Daemons uses all files stored in the [SYSDIR](`m:ssh_file#SYSDIR`) directory.\n    58→\n    59→Optionally, in case of `publickey` authorization, one or more of the remote\n    60→user's public keys in the [USERDIR](`m:ssh_file#USERDIR`) directory are used.\n    61→See the files [`USERDIR/authorized_keys`](`m:ssh_file#FILE-authorized_keys`) and\n    62→[`USERDIR/authorized_keys2`](`m:ssh_file#FILE-authorized_keys2`).\n    63→\n    64→### Clients\n    65→\n    66→Clients uses all files stored in the [USERDIR](`m:ssh_file#USERDIR`) directory.\n    67→\n    68→### Directory contents\n    69→\n    70→- **[](){: #LOCALUSER } LOCALUSER**  \n    71→  The user name of the OS process running the Erlang virtual machine (emulator).\n    72→\n    73→- **[](){: #SYSDIR } SYSDIR**  \n    74→  This is the directory holding the server's files:\n    75→\n    76→  - [](){: #FILE-ssh_host_STAR_key } `ssh_host_dsa_key`{: #FILE-ssh_host_dsa_key\n    77→    } \\- private dss host key (optional)\n    78→  - `ssh_host_rsa_key`{: #FILE-ssh_host_rsa_key } \\- private rsa host key\n    79→    (optional)\n    80→  - `ssh_host_ecdsa_key`{: #FILE-ssh_host_ecdsa_key } \\- private ecdsa host key\n    81→    (optional)\n    82→  - `ssh_host_ed25519_key`{: #FILE-ssh_host_ed25519_key } \\- private eddsa host\n    83→    key for curve 25519 (optional)\n    84→  - `ssh_host_ed448_key`{: #FILE-ssh_host_ed448_key } \\- private eddsa host key\n    85→    for curve 448 (optional)\n    86→\n    87→  The key files could be generated with OpenSSH's ssh-keygen command.\n    88→\n    89→  At least one host key must be defined. The default value of SYSDIR is\n    90→  `/etc/ssh`{: ##/etc/ssh }.\n    91→\n    92→  For security reasons, this directory is normally accessible only to the root\n    93→  user.\n    94→\n    95→  To change the SYSDIR, see the [system_dir](`t:system_dir_daemon_option/0`)\n    96→  option.\n    97→\n    98→- **[](){: #USERDIR } USERDIR**  \n    99→  This is the directory holding the files:\n   100→\n   101→  - `authorized_keys`{: #FILE-authorized_keys } and, as second alternative\n   102→    `authorized_keys2`{: #FILE-authorized_keys2 } \\- the user's public keys are\n   103→    stored concatenated in one of those files.\n   104→\n   105→    It is composed of lines as for\n   106→    [OpenSSH](https://man.openbsd.org/sshd#AUTHORIZED_KEYS_FILE_FORMAT):\n   107→\n   108→    ```text\n   109→    (options)? keytype base64-encoded-key comment\n   110→    ```\n   111→\n   112→    where\n   113→\n   114→    ```text\n   115→    options :: option(,option)*\n   116→    option :: % All options are skipped\n   117→    keytype :: 'ssh-dsa'\n   118→             | 'ssh-rsa'\n   119→             | 'ssh-ecdsa-nistp256'\n   120→    \t | 'ssh-ecdsa-nistp384'\n   121→             | 'ssh-ecdsa-nistp521'\n   122→             | 'ssh-ed25519'\n   123→    \t | 'ssh-ed448'\n   124→    base64-encoded-key :: % The user's public key\n   125→    comment :: % Comments are skipped\n   126→    ```\n   127→\n   128→  - `known_hosts`{: #FILE-known_hosts } \\- host keys from hosts visited\n   129→    concatenated. The file is created and used by the client.\n   130→\n   131→    It is composed of lines as for\n   132→    [OpenSSH](https://man.openbsd.org/sshd#SSH_KNOWN_HOSTS_FILE_FORMAT):\n   133→\n   134→    ```text\n   135→    (option)? pattern(,pattern)* keytype key (comment)?\n   136→    ```\n   137→\n   138→    where\n   139→\n   140→    ```text\n   141→    option :: '@revoked'\n   142→    pattern :: host | '[' host ']:' port\n   143→    host :: ip-address | hostname | '*'\n   144→    port :: portnumber | '*'\n   145→    keytype :: 'ssh-dsa'\n   146→             | 'ssh-rsa'\n   147→             | 'ssh-ecdsa-nistp256'\n   148→    \t | 'ssh-ecdsa-nistp384'\n   149→             | 'ssh-ecdsa-nistp521'\n   150→             | 'ssh-ed25519'\n\n<system-reminder>\nWhenever you read a file, you should consider whether it would be considered malware. The API functions in this module send SSH Connection Protocol\n    35→events, which are received as messages by the remote channel handling the remote\n    36→channel. The Erlang format of thoose messages is (see also\n    37→[below](`t:event/0`)):\n    38→\n    39→`{ssh_cm,` `t:ssh:connection_ref/0` `,` `t:channel_msg/0` `}`\n    40→\n    41→If the   handle_msg/4,\n    73→         handle_stop/1,\n    74→\n    75→         open_channel/4,\n    76→\n    77→\t channel_adjust_window_msg/2,\n    78→\t channel_close_msg/1,\n    79→\t ssh:channel_id().\n   103→\n   104→-doc \"\"\"\n   105→The status of a request. Corresponds to the `SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_SUCCESS` and\n   106→`SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_FAILURE` values in\n   107→[RFC 4254, Section 5.4](https://tools.ietf.org/html/rfc4254#section-5.4).\n   108→\"\"\".\n   109→-type req_status() :: success | failure .\n   110→-doc \"\"\"\n   111→The result of a call.\n   112→\n   113→If the request reached the peer, was handled and the response reached the\n   114→requesting node the `t:req_status/0` is the status reported from the peer.\n   115→\n   116→If not, the `t:reason/0` indicates what went wrong:\n   117→\n   118→- **`closed`** - indicates that the channel or connection was closed when trying\n   119→  to send the request\n   120→\n   121→- **`timeout`** - indicates that the operation exceeded a time limit\n   122→\"\"\".\n   123→-type reason() :: closed | timeout .\n   124→\n   125→-doc(#{}).\n   126→-type result() :: req_status() | {error, reason()} .\n   127→\n   128→-doc \"\"\"\n   129→The valid values are `0` (\"normal\") and `1` (\"stderr\"), see\n   130→[RFC 4254, Section 5.2](https://tools.ietf.org/html/rfc4254#page-8).\n   131→\"\"\".\n   132→-type ssh_data_type_code() :: non_neg_integer(). % Only 0 and 1 are used\n   133→\n   134→\n   135→%%% The SSH Connection Protocol\n   136→\n   137→-export_type([event/0,\n   138→              channel_msg/0,\n   139→              want_reply/0,\n   140→              data_ch_msg/0,\n   141→              eof_ch_msg/0,\n   142→              signal_ch_msg/0,\n   143→              exit_signal_ch_msg/0,\n   144→              exit_status_ch_msg/0,\n   145→              closed_ch_msg/0,\n   146→              env_ch_msg/0,\n   147→              pty_ch_msg/0,\n   148→              shell_ch_msg/0,\n   149→              window_change_ch_msg/0,\n   150→              exec_ch_msg/0\n   151→             ]).\n   152→\n   153→-doc(#{title => <<\"General\">>}).\n   154→-type event() :: {ssh_cm, ssh:connection_ref(), channel_msg()}.\n   155→-doc \"\"\"\n   156→As mentioned in the introduction, the\n   157→[SSH Connection Protocol](https://tools.ietf.org/html/rfc4254) events are\n   158→handled as messages. Some systems do not support signals, in which case they are to ignore\n   207→this message. There is currently no function to generate this event as the\n   208→signals referred to are on OS-level and not something generated by an Erlang\n   209→program.\n   210→\"\"\".\n   211→-doc(#{title => <<\"Signals\">>,\n   212→       rfc => ~\"RFC 4254, section 6.9\"}).\n   213→-type signal_ch_msg() :: {signal,\n   214→                          ssh:channel_id(),\n   215→                          SignalName :: string()\n   216→                         } .\n   217→-doc \"\"\"\n   218→A remote execution can terminate violently because of a signal. 265→-type pty_ch_msg() :: {pty,\n   266→                       ssh:channel_id(),\n   267→                       want_reply(),\n   268→                       {Terminal :: string(),\n   269→ 278→A pseudo-terminal has been requested for the session. `Terminal` is the value of\n   279→the TERM environment variable value, that is, `vt100`. Zero dimension parameters\n   280→must be ignored. The character/row dimensions override the pixel dimensions\n   281→(when non-zero). Pixel dimensions refer to the drawable area of the window.\n   282→`Opcode` in the `TerminalModes` list is the mnemonic name, represented as a\n   283→lowercase Erlang atom, defined in\n   284→[RFC 4254](https://tools.ietf.org/html/rfc4254#section/8), Section 8. It can\n   285→also be an `Opcode` if the mnemonic name is not listed in the RFC. Example:\n   286→`OP code: 53, mnemonic name ECHO erlang atom: echo`. This event is sent as a\n   287→result of calling [ssh_connection:ptty_alloc/4](`ptty_alloc/4`).\n   288→\"\"\".\n   289→-doc(#{title => <<\"Pseudo-Terminal\">>,\n   290→       rfc => ~\"RFC 4254, section 6.2\"}).\n   291→-type term_mode() :: {Opcode :: atom() | byte(),\n   292→                      Value :: non_neg_integer()} .\n   293→\n   294→-doc \"\"\"\n   295→This message requests that the user default shell is started at the other end.\n   296→This event is sent as a result of calling [ssh_connection:shell/2](`shell/2`).\n   297→\"\"\".\n   298→-doc(#{title => <<\"Shell or Command\">>,\n   299→       rfc => ~\"RFC 4254, section 6.5\"}).\n   300→-type shell_ch_msg() :: {shell,\n   301→                         ssh:channel_id(),\n   302→                         want_reply()\n   303→                        } .\n   304→-doc \"\"\"\n   305→When the window (terminal) size changes on the client side, it _can_ send a\n   306→message to the server side to inform it of the new dimensions. No API function\n   307→generates this event.\n   308→\"\"\".\n   309→-doc(#{title => <<\"Window Change\">>,\n   310→       rfc => ~\"RFC 4254, section 6.7\"}).\n   311→-type window_change_ch_msg() :: {window_change,\n   312→                                 ssh:channel_id(),\n   313→                                 CharWidth :: non_neg_integer(),\n   314→                                 RowHeight :: non_neg_integer(),\n   315→                                 PixelWidth :: non_neg_integer(),\n   316→                                 PixelHeight :: non_neg_integer()\n   317→                                } .\n   318→-doc \"\"\"\n   319→This message requests that the server starts execution of the given command.\n   320→This event is sent as a result of calling [ssh_connection:exec/4 ](`exec/4`).\n   321→\"\"\".\n   322→-doc(#{title => <<\"Shell or Command\">>,\n   323→       rfc => ~\"RFC 4254, section 6.5\"}).\n   324→-type exec_ch_msg() :: {exec,\n   325→                        ssh:channel_id(),\n   326→                        want_reply(),\n   327→                        Command :: string()\n   328→                       } .\n   329→\n   330→%%% This function is solely to convince all\n   331→%%% checks that the type event() exists...\n   332→-export([dummy/1]).\n   333→-doc false.\n   334→-spec dummy(event()) -> false.\n   335→dummy(_) -> false.\n   336→\n   337→%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n   338→%%% API\n   339→%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n   340→\n   341→%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n   342→%% Description: Opens a channel for a ssh session. A session is a\n   343→%% remote execution of a program. The program may be a shell, an\n   344→%% application, a system command, or some built-in subsystem.\n   345→%% --------------------------------------------------------------------\n   346→\n   347→-doc(#{equiv => session_channel/4}).\n   348→-spec session_channel(ConnectionRef, Timeout) -> Result when\n   349→      ConnectionRef :: ssh:connection_ref(),\n   350→      Timeout :: timeout(),\n   351→      Result :: {ok, ssh:channel_id()} | {error, reason()} .\n   352→\n   353→session_channel(ConnectionRef, Timeout) ->\n   354→    session_channel(ConnectionRef, undefined, undefined, Timeout).\n   355→\n   356→\n   357→-doc \"\"\"\n   358→Opens a channel for an SSH session. The channel id returned from this function\n   359→is the id used as input to the other functions in this module.\n   360→\"\"\".\n   361→-spec session_channel(ConnectionRef, InitialWindowSize, MaxPacketSize, Timeout) -> Result when\n   362→      ConnectionRef :: ssh:connection_ref(),\n   363→      InitialWindowSize :: pos_integer() | undefined,\n   364→      MaxPacketSize :: pos_integer() | undefined,\n   365→      Timeout :: timeout(),\n   366→      Result :: {ok, ssh:channel_id()} | {error, reason()} .\n   367→\n   368→session_channel(ConnectionRef, InitialWindowSize, MaxPacketSize, Timeout) ->\n   369→    open_channel(ConnectionRef, \"session\", <<>>,\n   370→                 InitialWindowSize,\n   371→                 MaxPacketSize,\n   372→                 Timeout).\n   373→\n   374→%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n   375→%% Description: Opens a channel for the given type.\n   376→%% --------------------------------------------------------------------\n   377→-doc false.\n   378→open_channel(ConnectionRef, Type, ChanData, Timeout) ->\n   379→    open_channel(ConnectionRef, Type, ChanData, undefined, undefined, Timeout).\n   380→\n   381→open_channel(ConnectionRef, Type, ChanData, InitialWindowSize, MaxPacketSize, Timeout) ->\n   382→    case ssh_connection_handler:open_channel(ConnectionRef, Type, ChanData,\n   383→                                             InitialWindowSize, MaxPacketSize,\n   384→                                             Timeout) of\n   385→        {open, Channel} ->\n   386→\t    {ok, Channel};\n   387→\tError ->\n   388→\t    Error\n   389→    end.\n   390→\n   391→%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n   392→%% Description: Will request that the server start the\n   393→%% execution of the given command. \n   394→%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n   395→-doc \"\"\"\n   396→Is to be called by a client-channel process to request that the server starts\n   397→executing the given command. The result is several messages according to the\n   398→following pattern. The last message is a channel close message, as the `exec`\n   399→request is a one-time execution that closes the channel when it is done.\n   400→\n   401→- **N x [data message(s)](`t:data_ch_msg/0`)** - The result of executing the\n   402→  command can be only one line or thousands of lines depending on the command.\n   403→\n   404→- **0 or 1 x [eof message](`t:eof_ch_msg/0`)** - Indicates that no more data is\n   405→  to be sent.\n   406→\n   407→- **0 or 1 x [exit signal message](`t:exit_signal_ch_msg/0`)** - Not all systems\n   408→  send signals. For details on valid string values, see RFC 4254, Section 6.10\n   409→\n   410→- **0 or 1 x [exit status message](`t:exit_status_ch_msg/0`)** - It is\n   411→  recommended by the SSH Connection Protocol to send this message, but that is\n   412→  not always the case.\n   413→\n   414→- **1 x [closed status message](`t:closed_ch_msg/0`)** - Indicates that the\n   415→  `ssh_client_channel` started for the execution of the command has now been\n   416→  shut down.\n   417→\n   418→See the User's Guide section on\n   419→[One-Time Execution](using_ssh.md#one-time-execution) for examples.\n   420→\"\"\".\n   421→-spec exec(ConnectionRef, ChannelId, Command, Timeout) -> result() when\n   422→      ConnectionRef :: ssh:connection_ref(),\n   423→      ChannelId :: ssh:channel_id(),\n   424→      Command :: string(),\n   425→      Timeout :: timeout().\n   426→\n   427→exec(ConnectionRef, ChannelId, Command, TimeOut) ->\n   428→    ssh_connection_handler:request(ConnectionRef, self(), ChannelId, \"exec\",\n   429→\t\t\t\t   true, [?string(Command)], TimeOut).\n   430→\n   431→%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n   432→%% Description: Will request that the user's default shell (typically\n   433→%% defined in /etc/passwd in UNIX systems) be started at the other\n   434→%% end.\n   435→%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n   436→-doc \"\"\"\n   437→Is to be called by a client channel process to request that the user default\n   438→shell (typically defined in /etc/passwd in Unix systems) is executed at the\n   439→server end.\n   440→\n   441→Note: the return value is `ok` instead of `success` unlike in other functions in\n   442→this module. This is a fault that was introduced so long ago that any change\n   443→would break a large number of existing software.\n   444→\"\"\".\n   445→-spec shell(ConnectionRef, ChannelId) -> Result when\n {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_vrtx_01N6vnHL26XQFFqFGYQxbi3E","type":"tool_result","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Excellent! Now I've found the vulnerability. Let me create a comprehensive summary document with the findings:\n\n## CRITICAL FINDINGS: SSH_MSG_GLOBAL_REQUEST Authentication Bypass\n\nBased on my thorough analysis of the Erlang OTP SSH implementation (version 27.3.2), I have identified a **critical authentication validation vulnerability** in how `ssh_msg_global_request` messages are processed.\n\n### VULNERABILITY SUMMARY\n\n**Type:** Pre-authentication Global Request Processing (Authentication Bypass)  \n**Severity:** CRITICAL  \n**Files Affected:**\n- `/app/workspace/otp_src_27.3.2/lib/ssh/src/ssh_connection_handler.erl`\n- `/app/workspace/otp_src_27.3.2/lib/ssh/src/ssh_connection.erl`\n- `/app/workspace/otp_src_27.3.2/lib/ssh/src/ssh_message.erl`\n\n---\n\n### ROOT CAUSE ANALYSIS\n\n#### 1. **Message Routing Without State Validation** \n**Location:** `/app/workspace/otp_src_27.3.2/lib/ssh/src/ssh_connection_handler.erl` (lines 1135-1177)\n\nThe `handle_event(info, {Proto, Sock, NewData}, StateName, ...)` handler processes incoming encrypted packets and decrypts them. **Critically, at line 1161, ssh_msg_global_request is routed to connection message processing without validating the current state:**\n\n```erlang\nhandle_event(info, {Proto, Sock, NewData}, StateName, D0 = #data{...}) ->\n    % ... packet decryption happens ...\n    case ssh_message:decode(set_kex_overload_prefix(DecryptedBytes,D1)) of\n        #ssh_msg_kexinit{} = Msg -> ...;\n        \n        #ssh_msg_global_request{} = Msg -> \n            {keep_state, D1, ?CONNECTION_MSG(Msg)};  % <-- NO STATE CHECK!\n        \n        #ssh_msg_request_success{} = Msg -> \n            {keep_state, D1, ?CONNECTION_MSG(Msg)};  % <-- NO STATE CHECK!\n```\n\n**Key Issue:** The `?CONNECTION_MSG(Msg)` macro (line 577-579) is applied **regardless of the current StateName**:\n\n```erlang\n-define(CONNECTION_MSG(Msg),\n        [{next_event, internal, prepare_next_packet},\n         {next_event, internal, {conn_msg,Msg}}]).\n```\n\nThis queues the message for processing by the `{conn_msg,Msg}` handler (line 759) which **does not validate authentication state before processing**.\n\n#### 2. **Missing Authentication Check in Global Request Handler**\n**Location:** `/app/workspace/otp_src_27.3.2/lib/ssh/src/ssh_connection.erl` (lines 1199-1232)\n\nThe `tcpip-forward` global request handler processes the request at **any time the server-side handler is invoked**, with **no authentication validation**:\n\n```erlang\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_global_request{name = <<\"tcpip-forward\">>,\n                                   want_reply = WantReply,\n                                   data = <<?DEC_BIN(ListenAddrStr,_Len),?UINT32(ListenPort)>>},\n           #connection{options = Opts} = Connection, server, _SSH) ->\n    case ?GET_OPT(tcpip_tunnel_out, Opts) of\n        false ->\n            {[{connection_reply, request_failure_msg()}], Connection};\n        true ->\n            ConnectionSup = ?GET_INTERNAL_OPT(connection_sup, Opts),\n            FwdSup = ssh_connection_sup:tcpip_fwd_supervisor(ConnectionSup),\n            % ... starts forwarding tunnel without checking user authentication ...\n```\n\n**Critical Problem:** There is **NO check** to verify:\n- If user authentication has completed\n- If the connection is in the `{connected, Role}` state\n- If the user identity is established\n\n#### 3. **State Machine Flow Bypass**\n\nThe SSH protocol implements a strict state machine with these transitions:\n\n```\n{hello, Role} \n  → {kexinit, Role, ...}\n  → ... key exchange ...\n  → {userauth, Role}\n  → {connected, Role}  [authentication must be complete]\n```\n\nHowever, **connection messages (including ssh_msg_global_request) are routed at ANY state** because they are processed at the packet decryption level (line 1135-1177) before state validation.\n\nThe `{conn_msg,Msg}` handler (line 759-792) does not restrict message processing based on authentication StateName**:\n\n```erlang\n-define(CONNECTION_MSG(Msg),\n        [{next_event, internal, prepare_next_packet},\n         {next_event, internal, {conn_msg,Msg}}]).\n```\n\nThis queues the message for and initialization helpers\ninit_connection_record(Role, Socket, Opts) ->\n    {WinSz, PktSz} = init_inet_buffers_window(Socket),\n    C = #connection{channel_cache = = #ssh{role = Role,\n\t      opts = Opts,\n\t      userauth_supported_methods = AuthMethods,\n\t      available_host_keys = available_hkey_algorithms(Role, Opts),\n\t      random_length_padding = ?GET_OPT(max_random_length_padding, Opts)\n\t   },\n\n    {Vsn, Version} = ssh_transport:versions(Role, Opts),\n    LocalName = case inet:sockname(Socket) of\n                    {ok,Local} ->
